# Results Index — FM-switching

Machine-readable provenance is embedded in each JSON as `_provenance`. This index is the human-readable cross-box correctness trail. One row per result file.

**Raw per-event files (since 2026-10-16):** per-token stamps, per-step decode times and metric timelines are committed **only** as `<name>.jsonl.gz`; token stamps are delta-encoded (`t0_ms` + integer `dt_ms`), lossless at their 1 ms recording precision. Harnesses append plain `.jsonl` during a run; compact with `python experiments/lib/rawio.py <file.jsonl>` (verifies the round trip, then removes the plain file) before committing. Read with `rawio.read_jsonl(path)`, which handles plain and gzipped forms.

## Naming schema

| type | pattern | example |
|---|---|---|
| Accuracy frontier | `frontier_<model>.json` | `frontier_qwen7b.json` |
| Per-question arrays | `frontier_<model>_perquestion.json` | `frontier_smollm2_perquestion.json` |
| Frame sweep | `framesweep_<model>.json` | `framesweep_qwen7b.json` |
| Inertia curve | `inertia_<model>_<device>.json` | `inertia_smollm2_jetson.json` |
| Premise / pilot | `premise_<model>_<tag>.json` | `premise_qwen7b_n150.json` |
| Historical sweep | `representation_sweep_<model>_<tag>.json` | `representation_sweep_qwen7b_n150.json` |
| Caches | `captions_cache.json`, `summaries_cache_80.json`, `summaries_cache_200.json` | — |

**Model slugs:** `qwen7b` = Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct · `smollm2` = HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct · `qwen3b` = Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct · `qwenvl3b` = Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct (or fine-tuned variant) · `qwenvl7b` = Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct · `mistral7b` = mistralai/Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.2  
**Device slugs:** `a6000` = NVIDIA RTX A6000 (flash, GPU 1) · `rtx3090ti` = NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3090 Ti (flash, GPU 0) · `jetson_orin` = Jetson AGX Orin (separate SSH host) · `jetson` = legacy alias for `jetson_orin` (pre-reorganization files only)

**Result validity flags:** Any result whose `status.rerun_required_before_downstream_use == true` must be re-run or redesigned before it feeds a figure, table, or the orchestration simulator. Currently flagged: `results/archive/frontier_findingdory_qwenvl3b.json` (OOD-confounded frame-budget slope — see its `status` block). The full-frame operating point (b=96, rm=0.565) is the only FindingDory number cleared for downstream use.

**Simulator pin:** `simulator/cost_model.py` imports these six files by path at load time and must not be moved:
`results/frontier_qwen7b.json`, `results/frontier_qwen7b_perquestion.json`,
`results/frontier_smollm2.json`, `results/frontier_smollm2_perquestion.json`,
`results/inertia_smollm2_a6000.json`, `results/inertia_smollm2_jetson.json`.

## Caches (tracked in git — content-addressed, not regenerated per run)

`captions_cache.json`, `summaries_cache_80.json`, `summaries_cache_200.json`,
`locomo_summaries_80.json`, and `locomo_summaries_200.json` **are tracked in git**.
They are not regenerated on every run; a run that needs a cached value reads it,
and a run that generates new entries appends to the cache. Do not gitignore them.

| file | content | note |
|---|---|---|
| `captions_cache.json` | VLM captions for 500 EgoSchema clips (Qwen2.5-VL-3B, 16 frames) | Extended from 150→500 during frontier_qwen7b run |
| `summaries_cache_80.json` | LLM summaries ~80 tok per clip for EgoSchema (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) | Reused by smollm2 run |
| `summaries_cache_200.json` | LLM summaries ~200 tok per clip for EgoSchema (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) | New during frontier_qwen7b run |
| `locomo_summaries_80.json` | LLM summaries ~80 tok per LoCoMo conversation (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) | Generated during locomo_audit_scaled run |
| `locomo_summaries_200.json` | LLM summaries ~200 tok per LoCoMo conversation (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) | Generated during locomo_audit_scaled run |

Mechanism confirmed: summaries fail by discarding the sparse fact, not by failing to reason. | new | new |
| `results/fidelity/frontier_infinithor_qwen7b.json` | `frontier_infinithor.py` | qwen7b | a6000 | 60 multi-clue (NsiEH), 65/219 traj_ids shipped on HuggingFace; conditions blind/summary-80/full; lazy LLM judge | COMPRESSIBLE — gate FAIL: full=0.58, summary-80=0.55, blind=0.36 (non-salient). Full beats blind +0.22 (history used, anchors recoverable). Full−summary-80 gap only +0.03 on non-salient (threshold >0.05 not met). Distance: NEAR gap=+0.18 (n=11), MID gap=0.00 (n=5), FAR gap=−0.08 (n=13) — summary outperforms full at far distance. Dispersion: gap carried entirely by 3 trajectories, 18/21 non-salient trajs show zero or negative gap. Interpretation: at 873–2,149 tokens (mean 1,441) templated oracle logs, summary-80 captures relevant events; compressible structured-log regime. Reduced gate / orientation audit, not a benchmark characterisation. | new | new |
| `results/fidelity/locomo_audit_scaled_qwen7b.json` | `locomo_audit_scaled.py` | qwen7b | a6000 | 282 cat=1 single-hop (full ceiling); conditions blind/window-10/summary-80/summary-200/full; omission judge=Qwen2.5-7B; evidence distance via dia_id annotations | INCOMPRESSIBLE BY OMISSION (scaled, confirmed): full=34.0% [28.8,39.8%], summary-80=9.9% [7.0,14.0%], gap=−24.1pp (p<0.001***), summary-200=9.9% (gap=−24.1pp, p<0.001***), window-10=22.0% (gap=−12.1pp, p<0.001***). Omission: 92.3% of gap cases (72/78) = gold absent from summary; 7.7% = model reasoning failure. Dispersion: gap spread across 78/282 questions; top-1=1.5%, top-3=4.4% of gap — not an outlier artifact. Distance: 96.5% FAR (>20 turns), full−s80 gap at FAR = +0.243; confirms structurally far-distance sparse-fact retrieval failure. | new | new |
| `results/archive/geolife_microgate_qwen7b.json` | `geolife_audit.py` (micro-gate) | qwen7b | a6000 | 123 non-salient sequential-recall questions; 20 rich weeks; Users 000+052 (GeoLife, urban Beijing); conditions blind/window-5/summary-80/summary-200/full; T1 predecessor/T2 successor/T4 transport-mode templates | INCOMPRESSIBLE — MICRO-GATE PASS: full=58.5%, summary-80=22.0%, gap=+36.6pp (p<0.0001). blind=0.0% (no prior leakage), window-5=0.8% (far-distance confirmed). Two compression mechanisms: entity omission (gold entity absent from summary, 50% of gap cases) + ordering omission (gold entity present but sequential adjacency not recoverable from unordered summary inventory, 50%). T4 transport-mode: full=100%, s80=0%, gap=+100pp. Contexts 251–728 tokens. Physical-world sequential-ordering incompressibility: summaries are unordered inventories, cannot answer what came before/after X or what mode was used for leg N. | new | new |

## E24b — Stressed coupling falsification (2026-08-19)

Script: `simulator/provisioning/sweep_b.py`. Inputs: Q-table (E05/E11/E13); costs (E21/E22); 3-edge topology; relative tau ∈ {0.80,0.90,0.95}; drift_rate ∈ {0,20}; L_init=8192 turn_rate=880. CPU only; no GPU.
Sweep: 5 capacity × 4 mobility × 3 regime_mix × 3 tau × 2 drift × 9 policies × 3 seeds = 9,720 runs.

| file | script | model | device | n | headline | source commit |

## E35 (E34b) — Corrected WARM Catch-up Latency and Maintenance Ordering (2026-08-23)

## E37b — A1 Ratio Resolution + E36 K2 Consequence Analysis (2026-08-24)

Analysis only. Inputs: committed E37 (jetson_orin_qwen3b.json) and E23 (jetson_orin_qwen7b.json).
Key finding: incr_warm ratio 0.593@1k→0.705@16k (median 0.684 across LoCoMo turns).
Device_only fails 46.5% of LoCoMo 1s queries at measured ratio.
K2 PASSES at measured device speed (gap ≈11pp). E36's K2 violation was a lower-bound artifact.

## E36f — N_eff-ranked Policy: S2 Re-verification (2026-08-24)

Script: `experiments/orchestration/e36f_neff.py`. CPU (no GPU). Adds neff_ranked policy (argmax N_eff = min(N_mem, N_accel)) to E36e's fleet simulation.
All constants identical to E36e; no new measurements.
S2: FAIL in 3 cells (kv=4.5,9,36 GiB, ti=5s; regret +6.8–9.9pp) — new ti=5s failure from dynamic-L mixed-fleet effect; E36e's kv=18GiB ti≥30s cells now PASS.
S3: PASS at all kv_caps (neff_ranked ≥ fp_ranked at ti=5s, gap +0.2–16.4pp).
Distinguishability: 0/16 cells selection-distinguishable; 11/16 outcome-distinguishable. Honest activation: 11/16.
Design rule: choose full at ti≤6s (N_accel(win10)=6 < N_eff(full)≥7); choose win10 at ti>crossover.

## E36h — Two-part Rule Ablation (2026-08-25)

Script: `experiments/orchestration/e36h_ablation.py`. CPU (no GPU). Adds always_full_mb, always_window_mb, footprint_ranked_mb baselines; renames oracle → greedy_upper. 12 policies, 2,304 runs.
Decomposition: best_fixed (no MB, no N_eff) → best_fixed_mb (MB only) → neff_marginal (both parts).
admission_gain = best_fixed_mb − best_fixed; representation_gain = neff_marginal − best_fixed_mb.
Mean admission_gain = +9.62 pp; mean representation_gain = +0.48 pp. rep_gain positive in 2/16 cells; zero in 14/16.

## Study B — Vision KV State Reduction (2026-08-30)

Script: `experiments/vision/study_b_vision_kv.py`. A6000 (cuda:1), qwenvl7b (Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct), bfloat16.
Three conditions: full retention (all N frames), windowed (k=3 most recent), summary (real generation call per frame, text context for query).
N sweep: {1, 3, 6, 12, 24, 36, 48}. N=2 reps per (N, condition). KV per-token cost: 57,344 B/token (identical to text, SC4 ratio=1.000).
Key finding: cost ordering differs from text case. Vision window (377 ms constant) << full (6,332 ms at N=48) << summary construction (27,854 ms at N=48). Text ordering is full < window < summary (warm-append dominates text). H4 confirmed for ordering/magnitudes; H3 confirmed only for per-token KV byte cost.

## Study C — Difficulty Scaling: 3B vs 7B VLM Accuracy (2026-08-30)

Script: `experiments/vision/study_c_difficulty.py`. A6000 (cuda:1), qwenvl7b + qwenvl3b (Qwen2.5-VL, bfloat16).
COCO val2017, person category. Difficulty = annotated person count: L1=1, L2=2-3, L3=4-7, L4=8+. 30 images/level, 3 reps each. Two prompt modes: direct (max 30 tokens) and stepwise chain-of-thought (max 512 tokens). Greedy decoding, fixed seed 42. Trial order randomized per model.
Key findings: (RQ1) stepwise token count scales monotonically with difficulty for both models (7B: 69→140 tok, 3B: 75→126 tok). (RQ2) accuracy gap is non-monotone and reverses at high difficulty in stepwise mode. (RQ3) chain-of-thought hurts both models at high difficulty; 7B-stepwise-L4=0.000. Neither the gap-widening nor the CoT-benefit hypothesis is supported.

## Study D — Reasoning Compute vs Parameters (partial; 2026-08-30)

## Study C Re-scoring — Tolerance-Based Accuracy and Error Analysis (2026-08-30)

Scripts: `experiments/vision/study_c_rescore.py` (CPU only, no new inference), `experiments/vision/study_c_rescore_plots.py` (plotting).
Input: `results/vision/study_c/study_c_trials.csv` (1,440 rows). All sanity checks PASS.
Key findings: near-zero exact-match accuracy at L3–L4 is substantially a scoring artifact — within-1 accuracy is 2–3× higher (e.g., 7B-direct-L3: exact=0.367, within-1=0.800). Models track count ordinally (pooled Spearman 0.70–0.88). Systematic undercount at L2–L3 (~1 person); massive stepwise undercount at L4 (~4.5 persons, CoT collapse). Difficulty axis is usable at L3 under within-1; exact-match should be retired for L3+.

## Study D2 — Reasoning Compute vs Parameters, Full 4-Model Run (2026-08-30)

Scripts: `experiments/vision/study_d2_thinking.py`. A6000 (cuda:1), bfloat16, greedy decoding. Same 120 COCO images as Study C/D. transformers 5.12.1 · torch 2.4.1+cu118. Main matrix: L1–L3 × 4 models × 3 reps. L4 probe: 10 images × 1 rep × 2 Thinking models (termination classification only).

Key finding: At L3 under within-1, 4B-Thinking (0.767) = 8B-Thinking (0.767) > 4B-Instruct (0.733) = 8B-Instruct (0.733). 4B-T beats 8B-I under exact-match (+0.067) at 62× latency cost (8693ms vs 140ms). Think-token IQR/median=1.84 at L3 — not predictable within the level. L4 probe: non_termination=20% (4B-T) / 30% (8B-T); verbose_bounded=0% for both — budget failure at L4 is always reasoning non-convergence, never post-answer verbosity.

## Study F — A6000 Diagnostic Arm (2026-08-31)

Scripts: `experiments/vision/study_f_a6000_diagnostic.py`. Device: **A6000 (cuda:1, 48 GB)**, bfloat16, flash-attn 2.6.3. torch 2.4.1+cu118 · transformers 5.12.1.

Part 1: Qwen3-VL-4B-I vs 8B-I decode rate under 3 conditions (C1 dynamic cache, C2 static cache, C3 torch.compile). Fixed decode 256 tokens, EOS suppressed, 5 reps. Roofline = A6000 768 GB/s ÷ weight bytes.  
Part 2: Qwen2.5-VL-7B phase-level memory at N=1,3,6,12 (Study B setup). Reset peak counters between phases.

Key: 4B separates from 8B on A6000 (48.5 vs 32.0 tok/s C1; 54.8 vs 35.0 C2); C3 failed both (flash-attn not traceable with fullgraph=True). Phase 2: weights ~97% of peak; vision delta <0.3%; KV matches Study B (ratio 1.000). A6000 N=6 peak = 17.4 GB.

## Study G — Transfer Cost (2026-08-31)

Script: `experiments/vision/study_g_transfer_cost.py`. Device: **A6000 (cuda:1, 48 GB)**, bfloat16, flash_attention_2. torch 2.4.1+cu118 · transformers 5.12.1. Model: Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct, Study B snapshot cc594898. Same 560×560 frames.

Seven representations (R1 PNG, R2a JPEG-85, R2b JPEG-60, R3 window-k3 JPEG-85, R4 pixel tensors, R5 vision embeddings, R6 KV analytical, R7 text summary) × N ∈ {1,3,6,12,24,48}. Network: `simulator/markov_network.py`, 4 profiles (campus/urban/indoor/harsh), 200 samples/cell, stall-and-resume. N_REPS=2. R6 KV extracted=False (DynamicCache.key_cache absent in transformers 5.12.1; size analytical only).

Key finding: R7 (text summary) dominates at p50 in all 24 (repr × profile) cells. R3 (window k=3) is the best non-summary representation for N≥6. R4/R5 (same-model-only) are dominated despite skipping vision encoding, because larger payloads outweigh the reconstruction savings. R6 KV (analytical: 1.1 GB at N=48) is uncompetitive on network cost. Dominance of R7 does not resolve the representation decision: R7 wins on latency and loses on semantic fidelity (LoCoMo gap ≈ −24 pp, Study E13/E20).

## Study G2 — Transfer Cost Clean Rerun (2026-08-31)

Script: `experiments/vision/study_g2_transfer_cost.py`. A6000 (cuda:1), qwenvl7b, bfloat16, flash_attention_2. Real Study C COCO images (120 PNGs, 560×560); LOW=L1+L2 (60 img, 0–2 persons), HIGH=L3+L4 (60 img, 3+ persons). Fixes three StudyG defects: D1 maintenance omitted, D2 synthetic frames, D3 fidelity claims. Two accountings: A (transfer+recon only) and B (maintenance_cumulative + f×p50_routing), f∈{1,2,5,10,25}. Key: under accounting B, R3 dominates at all tested f for N≥12; R7 never wins a majority of cells; crossover f >> 25 for N≥12. R7 maintenance at N=48 = 76s (LOW) / 94s (HIGH) vs R3 ≈ 29ms.

Second remote 4B-T×RICH 30.00% (−3.85pp vs local), d−c=+1.54pp CI [−0.32,+3.59] — K4 does NOT fire there only because the rule is a conjunction (1.54>1pp), but the CI still includes 0 so the result is null, and the larger gain is partly an artifact of a worse remote dragging the random baseline down. DEEPER FINDING: the evidence-distance ORACLE (perfect knowledge of the quantity N2 estimates) gains only +1.85pp (8B-I) / +0.57pp (4B-T) against an accuracy-oracle headroom of +11.27/+11.92pp — so coverage-based escalation is limited by the SIGNAL being near-orthogonal to which-arm-is-right, not by predictor quality. N2's coverage SIGNAL is confined, not contradicted. D4 coverage from sampled timestamps: RECENT 90.4%, RICH 100%. think_closed 516/520, budget_hit 4/520, 4 parse failures = the 4 budget hits (sensitivity: all-correct gives 30.77%, verdict unchanged). | 2026-10-15  BACKEND: SDPA (transformers 5.12.1 default; no attn_implementation set), NOT the flash_attention_2 used by study_i2/study_i3 — absolute lat_ms values are not comparable to those cost curves; ratios and accuracy unaffected. |

| `results/dashcam/study_e9/e9_results.json`, `e9_mechanism_verification.md` | `experiments/dashcam/study_e9_episodes.py`, `study_e9_report.py` (conda `fmtk`) | 4B-I / 32B-I Nexar validation score traces replayed offline (StudyD14 arms 4BI N1/N3, 32BI N1/N3/N9) | CPU (flash; no GPU, no server, no inference) | 300 clips (150 pos / 150 neg), 10,660 clip-seconds per arm; 5,184 escalation cells + 36 baselines; 2-fold threshold cross-fitting by clip seed 0; clip bootstrap 2,000 seed 0 | StudyE9-episodes (offline): BOTH pre-registered rules pass. Capability shortens episodes at N=1 only (escalation-32B N=1 uses fewer resolver invocations per clip-hour than the 4B counterfactual, paired CI excluding 0 at 2/3 alarm-FPR levels at trigger 5/10/20 percent, both load points) and REVERSES at N=9 (+63 to +302 per clip-hour) because that arm's 2.6-3.9 s latency exceeds what a 10 s episode can use. Escalation is efficient in 20 of 108 cells, cheapest at 11 percent of always-cloud's cloud work with AP within 0.0098 at every horizon <= 1 s. Dominant effect: one second of end-to-end latency costs about as much AP as one second of horizon (always-cloud 32B N=1 0.1473 -> 0.0748 at h=0), and under measured latency always-cloud 32B N=9 (0.0391) scores below device-only (0.0528). Re-escalation dominates the budget (median first-trigger-to-final-resolution ~22 s against a 10 s cap; 112-143 false dismissals before the event). AP reproduces StudyE5 Part G to three decimals on all 20 arm x horizon cells. `reports/study_e9_episodes.md` | 2026-11-11 |

MODEL SCORES ARE THE PNG SCORES; JPEG accuracy parity is PENDING StudyE10a, so this file is UNVALIDATED and no StudyE9 conclusion rests on it. d_cloud falls in 3 of 8 arm x load cells (32B N=3 @75% 2->1 s, 32B N=9 @25% 3->1 s, 32B N=9 @75% 4->3 s); decision rule 1 becomes MET in 3 of 24 cells that failed under (ii) and decision rule 2 is met in 27 of 72 cells against 18 under (ii), which supports the report's attribution of the N=9 reversal to transport latency. | `results/dashcam/study_e12/e12_results.json`, `e12_mechanism_verification.md` | `experiments/dashcam/study_e12_admission.py`, `study_e12_report.py` (conda `fmtk`) | 4B-I / 32B-I Nexar validation score traces replayed offline (StudyD14 arms 4BI N1, 32BI N1/N3) | CPU (flash; no GPU, no server, no inference) | 300 clips (150 pos / 150 neg), 10,660 clip-seconds per arm; 672 cells = 2 resolvers x 2 latency settings x 3 alarm FPR x 2 trigger rates x 4 budgets x 7 policies; P-random 20 seeds; 2-fold threshold and predictor cross-fitting by clip seed 0; clip bootstrap 2,000 seed 0 stratified by label (primary configuration only) | StudyE12-admission (offline): THE DEVICE SCORE ALONE IS THE ADMISSION SIGNAL, AND ON AP NO ADMISSION RANKING SEPARATES FROM RANDOM. The pre-registered rule is NOT met - neither P-trajectory nor P-trajectory-work beats P-score on AP at h = 0.5 s with a CI excluding 0 at 2 of 3 budgets for either resolver - and P-score itself beats P-random in only 1 of 6 resolver x budget cells. REAL NEGATIVE, not a design failure: the trajectory predictor does separate the training target out of sample (AUROC 0.667-0.734) and control (b) removes that separation. The reason, flagged before the sweep: at h = 0.5 s StudyE9's escalation-32B (0.0411) is already behind device-only (0.0416), the two endpoints are only 0.0005 apart at the pre-registered horizon, so nothing is available by INTERPOLATING between them - but selection is not interpolation and P-oracle at a quarter budget reaches 0.0461, +0.0045 above admit-none and +0.0050 above admit-all on 31% of its GPU-seconds, so the headroom is real and of the same order as the effect the rule asked for; what fails is that no causal policy finds any of it. What admission buys is cloud work and alarms: P-score reaches 0.0578 at h = 0 for 43% of admit-all's GPU-s and cuts false alarms from 115.6 to 79.7 per clip-hour at the same budget. Both negative controls pass; consistency 6/6 with admit-all and admit-none reproducing StudyE9 exactly. `reports/study_e12_admission.md` | 2026-11-11 |

| `results/orchestration/live2/frozen_test/offline_grid_analysis.json` | `experiments/orchestration/live2/offline_grid_analysis.py` | n/a (offline analysis of committed grid logs, CPU only) | flash (CPU only) | 13 cells at 16 devices, seeds 4 to 6, 5 arms; 65 robot questions per three-seed arm | StudyFROZEN-test offline analysis for D144 (report section 12). ROBOT MISSES ARE PREFILL, NOT SCHEDULING: waiting before release p50 0.10 to 0.52 s against a modelled history prefill of 2.12 s when on time and 3.93 to 6.42 s when missed. WARM STATE IS UNEXERCISED AND THE INSTRUMENTS AGREE: 0 prefix-cache hits against 5.2 to 5.6 M queries per cell, cached_tokens 0 on every robot request, every residency row cold; but `telemetry_cached_tokens` is NULL in 372 of 372 rows of all 13 cells, so the engine never contradicts the belief (a dead validation read, named with its write site). REUSE IS WITHIN A HISTORY, NOT ACROSS QUESTIONS: median 1 question per robot history, yet a CONSTRUCTED prefill-on-arrival of each 30 s batch would have had 97.8 to 98.7 percent of the history warm. WARMING IS A MEMORY PROBLEM: 3 to 4 percent of the GPU budget but 1.9x to 2.7x the 62,944-token pool and 1.1x to 1.5x the 27 GiB host tier. VALUE PER GPU-SECOND INVERTS THE PREMISE: dashcam 1.07, refspatial 0.61, realworldqa 0.27, robot EVAL 0.136, so a value-based shedding rule sheds robot recall first (units differ, so the ranking is a convention). DELIVERY STAMP AUDIT: runs use `forward_at=first`, so on-time charges TTFT only; stamping at completion flips 0 to 9 robot and 0 to 11 SS questions per cell and widens the robot spread from 2 to 9, against the baselines. THE OVER-CONTEXT REQUEST EXPLAINED: b7 and mooncake REJECT it; C3 rescues it as CLOUD at RECENT, charged 467 tokens while 375 image parts went on the wire (HTTP 400), so C3's representation is expressible in the cost and unexpressible in the payload. | new |

| `results/orchestration/live2/hybrid/policy_f6r_proofs.json` | `experiments/orchestration/live2/hybrid/policy_f6r_proofs.py` | n/a (decision-path proofs, DRY) | flash (CPU only) | 6 cells at 16 / 20 / 32 devices x seeds 0, 4, three-type LH-REQ, 1200 s | StudyPOLICY-F D144 ruling 4 proofs for hybrid-F6r against hybrid-F6. THE ROBOT-SIDE-UNCHANGED PROOF FAILS FOR THE CHEAP GATE: robot decisions move at 4 of 6 cells (2, 0, 0, 3, 12, 2) and the total reaches 10,369 at 32 devices seed 0, because `_c6_prepare` advances the policy-core mirror ONCE PER PERIOD by design, so skipping the single-shot-only periods changes the mirror's state at the next robot period. The deviations are NOT A SUBSET: at 32 devices seed 0 F6r makes a `c6_dev_recent_to_hold` that F6 never makes anywhere. So hybrid-F6r is a THIRD POLICY, not a faster hybrid-F6; only hybrid-F6rm (mirror advanced every period) can carry the robot-only label, and it saves nothing. CHARACTERISTIC DECISION HOLDS: c6_calls 20 to 52, c6_scorer_error 0, every call on a robot-bearing period. COST THE GATE WOULD BUY: mirror time per 1200 s cell falls from 16.9 to 153.7 s down to 0.15 to 1.4 s, a 98.9 to 99.4 percent cut. Neither arm runs live (D144 ruling 3; C6 is being redesigned). | new |
| `results/orchestration/live2/frozen_test/warm_state_offline.json` | `experiments/orchestration/live2/warm_state_offline.py` | n/a (offline simulation on committed traces, CPU only) | flash (CPU only) | 36 cells: 2 sizes x 3 capacities x 6 policies, 65 robot questions per cell (16 devices, seeds 4, 5, 6) | StudyFROZEN-test offline warm-state study (report section 15), design input for the C6 redesign. NOTHING MEASURED: trace-driven, times and costs from the committed StudyRH5 curves, policies and sub-committed capacities CONSTRUCTED. AT THE COMMITTED CAPACITY WARMING CLOSES THE ROBOT DEADLINE AND SELECTION ADDS NOTHING: 51 of 65 on time with no warming becomes 65 of 65 under every policy, P1-to-P3 gap ZERO, for 90 GPU-s per 1200 s cell (7.5 percent of budget). SELECTION ONLY PAYS UNDER PRESSURE: the P1-to-P3 gap is 0 / 5 / 11 questions at 1.0x / 0.5x / 0.25x capacity, and a two-rate hazard plus the session's declared end (P2, fitted on seed 0, scored on 4, 5, 6 per D6) captures 4 of the 5 at 0.5x and 1 of the 11 at 0.25x. LRU THRASHES: its warming cost rises 271 to 1,774 to 2,431 GPU-s as capacity tightens while the questions saved fall 14 to 9 to 3; at 0.25x that is 68 percent of the GPU budget for 3 questions. 18 DEVICES IS THE SAME TABLE: 40:40:20 gives SEVEN robot devices at both 16 and 18, so 18 buys zero extra robot questions. CONTROL: P0 modelled 51 of 65 against measured 46 to 50, optimistic by the queueing and decode it excludes. LIMIT: the median history is asked ONE question, so this is sparse one-shot demand prediction, not caching for reuse. MEASURED CONTROL ADDED 2026-11-20 (section 15.4), from the edf and llf runs, the first arms in this study that emit history builds: warm robot TTFT p50 0.384 and 0.423 s at 96.6 percent cached against cold 2.44 to 3.10 s, so the model is conservative on the warm side; BUT edf has the WORST robot on-time of the four arms (5 of 20 against b7's 14 and hybrid-F's 15) because its 359 builds cost 211 s of build TTFT and its questions then WAIT 5.5 s at the median before release, longer than the whole 5 s deadline. Warming moves the binding resource from prefill to release, which is the one term this simulation explicitly does not model. Also a SCOPE CORRECTION to section 12.2: no robot question was served warm in the five frozen-grid arms, and that is not true of the grid now that edf and llf have run. | new |
| `results/orchestration/live2/frozen_test/warm_state_contention.json` | `experiments/orchestration/live2/warm_state_contention.py` | n/a (offline simulation, calibrated on committed live cells, CPU only) | flash (CPU only) | 19 cells: 3 capacities x (P0 plus P1/P2/P3 x W1/W2), 65 robot and 1,070 single-shot questions each (16 devices, seeds 4, 5, 6) | StudyFROZEN-test warm-state part 2 (report section 16): warming charged to the SAME GPU as deadline work, two disciplines (W1 warm on arrival in one FIFO, W2 warm only into spare capacity with an at-risk pause). CALIBRATION: interference factor f fitted on the two WARMING-FREE arms (dashcam 3.18, SS 1.47, robot 1.38 times the committed curve) and c = 5 slots taken from measured concurrency; edf and llf are pure validation, where dashcam transfers within 1 percent and llf robot within 5, while the WARM side misses badly (warm SS x0.27, build x0.65), so every contention number is a LOWER BOUND. AT THE COMMITTED CAPACITY THERE IS NO CONTENTION TO MANAGE: 65 of 65 robot on time under every policy and both disciplines, 10 of 65 questions queue at all, worst wait 63 ms, W1 and W2 indistinguishable. UNDER MEMORY PRESSURE THE DISCIPLINE PAYS ON SINGLE-SHOT, NOT ROBOTS: at 0.5x, W2 buys 1 robot and 7 SS questions and cuts the worst SS queue wait from 3.746 s to 1.589 s against a 2 s deadline; at 0.25x, 9 SS questions. CONTENTION IS A SYMPTOM OF THRASHING: warming GPU-s rise 271 to 1,764 to 2,433 as memory tightens because evicted histories are re-prefilled. SELECTION DOMINATES DISCIPLINE: LRU to hazard is worth 7 to 8 robot questions at 0.5x, W1 to W2 is worth 1. Model gives P0 43 of 65 robot against 46 to 50 measured, so read columns against each other, never as levels. | new |

| `results/orchestration/live2/frozen_test/cost_model_gate.json` | `experiments/orchestration/live2/cost_model_gate.py` | n/a | flash (CPU only) | 345,066 committed requests over 26 cells, 1,797 warm | **The D151 (6) cost-model gate FAILS and v5 does not go live on this evidence.** First token predicts out of sample at 0.94 to 1.07; DECODE OVER-PREDICTS BY 25 TO 32 PERCENT in every held-out class. The cause is not scarcity: WARM IS CONFOUNDED WITH ARM. edf n16 s4 has 358 gpu beliefs and 512,992 engine prefix hits, llf 362 and 424,224, while b7 and hybrid-F-lhreq have ZERO of each. Arms that rebuild histories go warm; arms that do not, never do. So the warm coefficient is fitted entirely on edf and llf and applied to arms that never go warm, which is why the by-arm split has zero held-out warm rows. More cells of the same arms cannot fix it; only an arm that goes warm and cold under one policy can, which is WARM-ALL + LRU. | new |

Step 4 control: `covex-v6-base` with the switch ON gives af8befb9420ad86a / 12,085, its own OFF digest and its committed value, so an arm that never enters `V6StateArm` collapses as it must. Step 2b: the switch arrives as a `bool` `True` in the INSTANCE dict of all five rung-2 arms and `_qcache_key_fix_resolved`, written only by `_queued_behind`, shows it was READ. **PROOF (b), THE DRY DECISION DELTA: THE DEFECT IS NOT HARMLESS TO AN ARM'S OWN DECISIONS.** 17 of 20 rung-2 cells are byte-identical ON against OFF, and **3 differ: `covex-v6-state-shares`, `-r1` and `-r2` at SEED 6, 35 rows setwise and 696 positionally, 12,465 rows OFF against 12,464 ON**. Stale-hit census per OFF cell at seed 4: 275 to 305 stale hits of 61,048 to 63,985 `_queued_behind` calls, of which only 5 to 9 had a non-empty cache and 5 to 7 returned a non-empty list to `harm_warm` (a stale hit whose list is empty cannot change a harm sum and is counted apart); **the ON cells have ZERO stale hits**. **PROOF (c), THE CROSS-ARM RECONCILIATION, NOT CONFIRMED.** The claim offered elsewhere was that the defect damages CROSS-ARM byte-identity proofs rather than an arm's own decisions. `covex-v6-state-shares-off`, the DECLARED Step-4 collapse of `covex-v6-state-uo` and exactly the switch-off-wrapper-against-base shape, is byte-identical to `covex-v6-state-uo` at all four seeds **with the switch OFF as well as ON**, so the pair shows no cross-arm damage to repair and cannot distinguish the two explanations: UNEXERCISED on this pair, neither confirmed nor refuted. The 575-decision observation it was offered to explain was on the `live2_cf_protect_defer` wrapper, which is not in any reachable commit, so it is not reproducible from the tree. **DISAGREEMENT WITH A PRIOR COMMITTED MEASUREMENT, declared under consistency check 1**: `d179_recall_skip_diagnosis.json` reports ZERO differing rows on all 12 rung-2 cells with the key as the only difference. This run reproduces zero on 17 of 20 and finds a difference at seed 6 on the shares family. Two differences in instrumentation account for it and the direction is the one that matters: that measurement applied the candidate fix through a WRAPPER installed on BOTH sides, and the wrapper allocates, so it perturbs the very address reuse under test, whereas here the committed key expression is switched in place and the OFF side reproduces the committed digest; and that measurement predates `covex-v6-state-shares-r2` and ran on a different tree. A wrapped measurement UNDERSTATES this defect. Step 3 is readable by hand twice over: the isolated trace shows the OFF key is an integer address that does NOT change between now_s 10.0 and 11.0 while the ON key is `('e1', 10.0)` then `('e1', 11.0)`, and a deliberately reoccupied address returns the previous period's list with the switch OFF and not with it ON; and inside a cell, a stale hit at t = 239.5 s asking about `robo000|s000` returns a row written 0.1 s earlier in a DIFFERENT epoch (`recall_arrival` against `stream_arrival`) for `opp0002312` of `asst001|ss0023`, carrying `slack_s` 1.841 computed from the other period's `now_s`. **REGISTER, stated with the numbers and not after them: a DRY REPLAY MEASURES DRY ALLOCATION PATTERNS.** A live run has a real backend, I/O threads and a different allocation history; nothing here bounds the live effect, and the dry number must not stand in for it. Because the defect moved an arm's OWN decisions on one of four seeds, the rerun with the switch ON must rerun the rung-2 arm AND its comparators together, at every seed that will be read; it is not safe to reuse a committed comparator cell. | new |

Net at s4: robot on time 95 to 79, and the loss is in NOT DELIVERED (35 to 49), not in LATE (2 to 4), so the pre-registered harm bound of at most 10 late HOLDS at every seed (4 / 2 / 4). Check 2 retention 0.7229 to 0.5301 at s4 and IDENTICAL at s6 (92 on-time both). **TWO CLAIMS KEPT APART: the register fix remains CORRECT ON ITS OWN TERMS** (the D162 regressor is built over submit-to-first-token intervals at `cost_model_gate_d162.py:97`, which is `self._w.total()` and not `_uncached_in_flight`) **while being REFUTED AS A LEVER FOR CHECK 2**; it is NOT recommended on correctness grounds alone because it made s4 worse, and that trade is the user's call. **r1 is the best shares version on the evidence in these cells.** Positive controls: PC1 reproduces committed `score.score.sweep[0]` `robot_pool_on_time` and `robot_pool_n` on all 6 cells (95/79, 113/113, 92/92), and `score.sweep` is recorded as reading None so the wrong path cannot pass as a zero; the r1 size/queue split reproduces the committed 9/25 at s4 and 7/5 at s5 exactly; the retention formula reproduces the committed 0.7229 before being applied to r2; the D162 price arithmetic reproduces `est_first_token_s` on all 140 skip rows with the module's own gate-drift check passing; the event lists are proved UNCAPPED (length equals `release_infeasible_events_total`). Registers: the per-reason W RECONSTRUCTION is reported as SECONDARY and carries NO headline, because its replay matches the logged `w_total` on only 59 to 61 percent of gate calls (the residual is the drain INSTANT, which no log carries); `run_wide_at_every_gate_call` is a CENSUS, `top_quartile` is a stratum with its size. **LIVE-TRAJECTORY VARIANCE BETWEEN r1 AND r2 AT THE SAME SEED IS NOT MEASURED**, so a same-seed delta is not automatically causal; bounding it needs at least three repeat cells of one unchanged arm at one seed, which do not exist for `-r1` or `-r2`. | new |
